\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\cdot)E_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p},t}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

We note from the first square that

E_{\mathbf{a},t}\in\Mat_{d}(K[\tau])\cap\Mat_{d}(B_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mathrm{perf}}[\tau])=\Mat_{d}(B_{\mathfrak{p}}[\tau]),

and thus
E_{\mathbf{a}}
is defined over
B_{\mathfrak{p}}
. A short calculation then yields

(4.4.10)

\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muE}_{\mathbf{a},t}=E_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p},t},

where
\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muE}_{\mathbf{a}}
is the reduction of
E_{\mathbf{a}}
modulo
\mathfrak{p}
as in §
4.4
. Since the rank of
E_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}
is the same as the rank of
E_{\mathbf{a}}
, we see that
E_{\mathbf{a}}
has good reduction at
\mathfrak{p}
.
∎

Corollary 4.4.11
.

Let
\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}_{f}
with
\deg\mathbf{a}>0
, and let
E_{\mathbf{a}}:\mathbf{A}\to\Mat_{d}(K[\tau])
be constructed as in Theorem
4.3.29
. Then there is a finite set of primes
\Sigma
of
B
, containing the primes above
f
, such that
E_{\mathbf{a}}
has good reduction modulo
\mathfrak{p}\notin\Sigma
.

Proof.

As in the proof of Theorem
4.3.29
, we obtain a model for
E_{\mathbf{a}}
over
K
by selecting a normalized
\overline{k}[\sigma]
-basis
\{h_{(a,j)}\mid(a,j)\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{a}}\}
of
H_{\mathbf{a}}(\overline{k})
defined over
K
. We can therefore find a set of primes
\Sigma
of
B
, containing the primes above
f
, such that for
\mathfrak{p}\notin\Sigma
, we have
h_{(a,j)}\in B_{\mathfrak{p}}[t,z]
for all
(a,j)\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{a}}
. Moreover, by possibly increasing
\Sigma
, we find for
\mathfrak{p}\notin\Sigma
that
\{\overline{h}_{(a,j)}\mid(a,j)\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{a}}\}
satisfy conditions (
4.1.7
)–(
4.1.9
) in §
4.1
modulo
\mathfrak{p}
. (There is a subtle point here, noted after (
4.1.8
), that we need
\overline{W}_{\mathbf{a}}^{(1)}
and
\overline{\Xi}_{\mathbf{a}}
to have disjoint supports modulo
\mathfrak{p}
, but having common supports can only happen for finitely many primes.) The reduction map

H_{\mathbf{a}}(\overline{k})\cap B_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mathrm{perf}}[t,z]\to H_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})

is surjective and commutes with
\sigma
. For
\mathfrak{p}\notin\Sigma
, the normalization of
\{\overline{h}_{(a,j)}\}
ensures it generates
H_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})
as an
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}[\sigma]
-module. Proposition
4.4.8
completes the proof.
∎

Remark 4.4.12
.

It is a natural question to characterize primes of good reduction for
E_{\mathbf{a}}
. Although Corollary
4.4.11
is sufficient for our main results on
L
-values, Theorem
5.3.15
and Corollary
5.3.17
, we show in §
A
that for each prime
\mathfrak{p}
of
B
not dividing
f
,
E_{\mathbf{a}}
is isomorphic over
K
to a model over
B_{\mathfrak{p}}
with good reduction modulo
\mathfrak{p}
. See Corollary
A.1.17
.

5.
Goss and Hecke
L
-functions

We now consider Goss
L
-functions attached to Sinha modules and identities for them in terms of Hecke
L
-functions for Anderson’s Hecke character. Our main results focus on special values of these
L
-functions at
s=0
.

5.1.
L
-functions for Sinha modules

We fix effective
\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}_{f}
with
\deg\mathbf{a}>0
, and let
E_{\mathbf{a}}:\mathbf{A}\to\Mat_{d}(K[\tau])
be its associated Sinha module defined over
K
as in §
4
. We let
\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}}
denote a finite set of primes of
B
, containing all primes above
f
, such that
E_{\mathbf{a}}
has good reduction at
\mathfrak{p}\notin\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}}
as in Corollary
4.4.11
. As in §
2.4
, in order to define the Goss
L
-functions for
E_{\mathbf{a}}
, we need to analyze the Galois action on its associated Tate modules.

Fix a prime
\mathfrak{p}\notin\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}}
, and choose a model

E_{\mathbf{a}}:\mathbf{A}\to\Mat_{d}(B_{\mathfrak{p}}[\tau])

for which
E_{\mathbf{a}}
has good reduction at
\mathfrak{p}
. Then as in (
4.4.10
), we have
\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muE}_{\mathbf{a}}\cong E_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}
over
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}
.

Let
\lambda\in\mathbf{A}_{+}
be irreducible with
\mathfrak{p}\nmid\lambda(\theta)
. Following §
2.4
, we let
\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}\in\Gal(K^{\mathrm{sep}}/K)
be a Frobenius element and set

(5.1.1)

\displaystyle P_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}(X)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Char(\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}},T_{\lambda}(E_{\mathbf{a}}),X)|_{t=\theta}\in A_{\lambda(\theta)}[X],

\displaystyle P_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}(X)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Char(\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}},T_{\lambda}(E_{\mathbf{a}})^{\vee},X)|_{t=\theta}\in k_{\lambda(\theta)}[X],

where
T_{\lambda}(E_{\mathbf{a}})
is the
\lambda
-adic Tate module of
E_{\mathbf{a}}
and
T_{\lambda}(E_{\mathbf{a}})^{\vee}
is its dual. As one may expect since we are in the situation of good reduction,
T_{\lambda}(E_{\mathbf{a}})
and
T_{\lambda}(E_{\mathbf{a}})^{\vee}
form families of strictly compatible representations in the sense of
[
29
]
*Def. 8.6.5. We prove in Proposition
5.1.4
that these polynomials are independent of the choice of
\lambda
, and furthermore
P_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}(X)\in A[X]
,
P_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}(X)\in k[X]
. For Drinfeld modules with good reduction, such results were proved originally by Takahashi
[
52
]
(see also
[
29
]
*Ex. 8.6.6.2), and our proof is similar. However, using results from
[
33
]
we also obtain more detailed information.

The reduction map
\pi:H_{\mathbf{a}}(B_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mathrm{perf}})\to H_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})
from Proposition
4.4.7
also commutes with the operation of
\mathbf{A}
, and moreover, as in §
4.2
, we can pick a
\overline{k}[t]
-basis of
H_{\mathbf{a}}(\overline{k})
,
\{n_{1},\dots,n_{r}\}\subseteq B_{\mathfrak{p}}^{(1)}[t,z]
, such that their reductions
\{\overline{n}_{1},\dots,\overline{n}_{r}\}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}[t,z]
form a
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}[t]
-basis of
H_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})
. Letting
\mathbf{n}=(n_{1},\dots,n_{r})^{{\mathsf{T}}}
and
\overline{\mathbf{n}}=(\overline{n}_{1},\dots,\overline{n}_{r})^{{\mathsf{T}}}
, we see that the matrix
\Phi
from (
4.2.9
) can be chosen in
\Mat_{r}(B_{\mathfrak{p}}[t])
, and satisfies,

\sigma\mathbf{n}=\Phi\mathbf{n},\quad\sigma\overline{\mathbf{n}}=\overline{\Phi}\overline{\mathbf{n}},

where
\overline{\Phi}\in\Mat_{r}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}[t])
is the reduction of
\Phi
modulo
\mathfrak{p}
. Moreover, the matrix
Q
from §
4.2
satisfies
Q\in\Mat_{r}(B_{\mathfrak{p}}^{(1)}[t])
, and

(5.1.2)

\Phi=Q^{(-1)}\Phi_{\mathbf{a}}Q^{-1},\quad\overline{\Phi}=\overline{Q}^{(-1)}\overline{\Phi}_{\mathbf{a}}\overline{Q}^{-1}.

Recall Anderson’s Hecke character
\chi_{\mathbf{a}}:\mathcal{I}_{K,f}\to K^{\times}
from §
3.4
. For convenience define

(5.1.3)

\psi_{\mathbf{a}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\chi_{-\mathbf{a}}=\chi_{\mathbf{a}}^{-1}:\mathcal{I}_{K,\mathfrak{f}}\to K^{\times},

which is a Hecke character of infinity type
\Theta_{-\mathbf{a}}=-\Theta_{\mathbf{a}}
as in §
3.4
.

Proposition 5.1.4
.

Let
\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}_{f}
be effective with
\deg\mathbf{a}>0
. Let
\mathfrak{p}
be a prime of
B
,
\mathfrak{p}\notin\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}}
. Then

P_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}(X)=\prod_{b\in(A/fA)^{\times}}\bigl(X-\chi_{\mathbf{a}}^{\rho_{b}}(\mathfrak{p})\bigr)\in A[X],\quad P_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}(X)=\prod_{b\in(A/fA)^{\times}}\bigl(X-\psi_{\mathbf{a}}^{\rho_{b}}(\mathfrak{p})\bigr)\in k[X].

These characteristic polynomials are independent of the choice of
\lambda\in\mathbf{A}_{+}
from (
5.1.1
).

Proof.

Pick
\lambda\in\mathbf{A}_{+}
irreducible such that
\mathfrak{p}\nmid\lambda(\theta)
. Since
\mathfrak{p}
is a prime of good reduction for
E_{\mathbf{a}}
and
\mathfrak{p}\nmid\lambda(\theta)
, there is an isomorphism of Tate modules,
T_{\lambda}(E_{\mathbf{a}})\to T_{\lambda}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muE}_{\mathbf{a}})
, compatible with the Galois action. If
\ell=[\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}:\mathbb{F}_{q}]
, by the definition in (
5.1.1
),

P_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}(X)=\Char(\tau^{\ell},T_{\lambda}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muE}_{\mathbf{a}}),X)|_{t=\theta}=\Char(\tau^{\ell},T_{\lambda}(E_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}),X)|_{t=\theta},

where the second equality follows from the isomorphism
\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muE}_{\mathbf{a}}\cong E_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}
over
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}
. On the other hand it follows from
[
33
]
*Eq. (3.7.1), Cor. 3.7.3 (with
Z=\emptyset
) (cf.
[
49
]
*Prop. 7) that

(5.1.5)

\Char(\tau^{\ell},T_{\lambda}(E_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}),X)=\Char\bigl(\overline{\Phi}^{(-\ell+1)}\cdots\overline{\Phi}^{(-1)}\overline{\Phi},X\bigr)\in\mathbf{A}[X].

Strictly speaking, the results in
[
33
]
*§3.7 do not appear to immediately imply this identity, as the proofs there center on
t
-motives rather than
t
-comotives. It is possible to rewrite these results from the standpoint of
t
-comotives instead, but there is a more straightforward explanation. Maurischat
[
38
]
has shown that abelian and coabelian are equivalent conditions for Anderson
t
-modules. By Hartl and Juschka
[
30
]
*Thm. 2.5.13, this implies that if
\overline{\Gamma}\in\Mat_{r}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}[t])
represents multiplication by
\tau
on the
t
-motive of
E_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}
, then there is
V\in\GL_{r}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}[t])
such that
\overline{\Gamma}^{{\mathsf{T}}}V=V^{(-1)}\overline{\Phi}
(see also
[
39
]
*Eq. (4.65)). In
[
33
]
*§3.7 it is shown that
\Char(\tau^{\ell},T_{\lambda}(E_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}),X)=\Char(\overline{\Gamma}^{(\ell-1)}\cdots\overline{\Gamma}^{(1)}\overline{\Gamma},X)
. As matrices with entries in
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}[t]
, it follows that
V^{(\ell)}=V
and
\overline{\Phi}^{(\ell)}=\overline{\Phi}
, and so

\bigl(\overline{\Gamma}^{(\ell-1)}\cdots\overline{\Gamma}^{(1)}\overline{\Gamma}\bigr)^{{\mathsf{T}}}=\overline{\Gamma}^{{\mathsf{T}}}\bigl(\overline{\Gamma}^{{\mathsf{T}}}\bigr)^{(1)}\cdots\bigl(\overline{\Gamma}^{{\mathsf{T}}}\bigr)^{(\ell-1)}\\
=V^{(-1)}\overline{\Phi}\,\overline{\Phi}^{(1)}\cdots\overline{\Phi}^{(\ell-1)}\bigl(V^{-1}\bigr)^{(\ell-1)}=V^{(-1)}\overline{\Phi}\,\overline{\Phi}^{(-\ell+1)}\cdots\overline{\Phi}^{(-1)}\bigl(V^{(-1)}\bigr)^{-1}.

Thus
\overline{\Gamma}^{(\ell-1)}\cdots\overline{\Gamma}^{(1)}\overline{\Gamma}
and
\overline{\Phi}\,\overline{\Phi}^{(-\ell+1)}\cdots\overline{\Phi}^{(-1)}
have the same characteristic polynomial, and the latter has the same characteristic polynomial as
\overline{\Phi}^{(-\ell+1)}\cdots\overline{\Phi}^{(-1)}\overline{\Phi}
, justifying (
5.1.5
).

Now
\overline{\Phi}=\overline{Q}^{(-1)}\overline{\Phi}_{\mathbf{a}}\overline{Q}^{-1}
, where
\overline{Q}\in\GL_{r}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}[t])
. It follows that
\overline{\Phi}^{(-\ell+1)}\cdots\overline{\Phi}^{(-1)}\overline{\Phi}=\overline{Q}^{(-1)}\overline{\Phi}_{\mathbf{a}}^{(-\ell+1)}\cdots\overline{\Phi}_{\mathbf{a}}^{(-1)}\overline{\Phi}_{\mathbf{a}}(\overline{Q}^{(-1)})^{-1}
, and thus

P_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}(X)=\Char\bigl(\overline{\Phi}_{\mathbf{a}}^{(-\ell+1)}\cdots\overline{\Phi}_{\mathbf{a}}^{(-1)}\overline{\Phi}_{\mathbf{a}},X\bigr)\big|_{t=\theta}=\prod_{b\in(A/fA)^{\times}}\biggl(X-\prod_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\overline{g}_{b\star\mathbf{a}}^{(i)}\biggr)\bigg|_{\xi},

with the second equality following from Proposition
4.2.6
(a). By the definition of
\chi_{\mathbf{a}}
from §
3.4
and Lemma
3.4.9
, we see that

P_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}(X)=\prod_{b\in(A/fA)^{\times}}\bigl(X-\chi_{b\star\mathbf{a}}(\mathfrak{p})\bigr)=\prod_{b\in(A/fA)^{\times}}\bigl(X-\chi_{\mathbf{a}}^{\rho_{b}}(\mathfrak{p})\bigr).

Finally the result for
P_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}(X)
follows directly from this one and the definition of
\psi_{\mathbf{a}}
.
∎

As in §
2.4
, we define reciprocal polynomials
Q_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}(X)
and
Q_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}(X)
for
P_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}(X)
and
P_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}(X)
and Goss
L
-functions, for
s\in\mathbb{Z}
,

(5.1.6)

L(E_{\mathbf{a}}/K,\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}},s)=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\notin\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}}}Q_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}\bigl(\mathcal{N}(\mathfrak{p})^{-s}\bigr)^{-1},\ L(E_{\mathbf{a}}^{\vee}/K,\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}},s)=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\notin\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}}}Q_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}\bigl(\mathcal{N}(\mathfrak{p})^{-s}\bigr)^{-1}.

When they converge, these
L
-functions take values in
k_{\infty}
. As defined these
L
-values depend on the model
E_{\mathbf{a}}
we have chosen.

Remark 5.1.7
.

We will show in Corollary
A.1.17
that
E_{\mathbf{a}}
has good reduction at all primes
\mathfrak{p}\nmid f
, so it makes sense to define

(5.1.8)

L(E_{\mathbf{a}}/K,s)=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\nmid f}Q_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}\bigl(\mathcal{N}(\mathfrak{p})^{-s}\bigr)^{-1},\quad L(E_{\mathbf{a}}^{\vee}/K,s)=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\nmid f}Q_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}\bigl(\mathcal{N}(\mathfrak{p})^{-s}\bigr)^{-1},

though there may not be a single model for
E_{\mathbf{a}}
over
B
with good reduction at every
\mathfrak{p}\nmid f
.

We also have Hecke
L
-functions as in §
2.4
, for
s\in\mathbb{Z}
,

(5.1.9)

L(\chi_{\mathbf{a}},s)=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\nmid f}\biggl(1-\frac{\chi_{\mathbf{a}}(\mathfrak{p})}{\mathcal{N}(\mathfrak{p})^{s}}\biggr)^{-1},\quad L(\psi_{\mathbf{a}},s)=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\nmid f}\biggl(1-\frac{\psi_{\mathbf{a}}(\mathfrak{p})}{\mathcal{N}(\mathfrak{p})^{s}}\biggr)^{-1}.

When they converge, these Hecke
L
-functions take values in
k_{\infty}(\zeta)
. We further set

L(\chi_{\mathbf{a}},\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}},s)=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\notin\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}}}\biggl(1-\frac{\chi_{\mathbf{a}}(\mathfrak{p})}{\mathcal{N}(\mathfrak{p})^{s}}\biggr)^{-1},\quad L(\psi_{\mathbf{a}},\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}},s)=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\notin\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}}}\biggl(1-\frac{\psi_{\mathbf{a}}(\mathfrak{p})}{\mathcal{N}(\mathfrak{p})^{s}}\biggr)^{-1}.

The restricted
L
-function
L(\chi_{\mathbf{a}},\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}},s)
differs from
L(\chi_{\mathbf{a}},s)
by finitely many Euler factors.

Proposition 5.1.10
.

Let
\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}_{f}
be effective with
\deg\mathbf{a}>0
, and let
D_{\mathbf{a}}=\deg(\mathbf{a})/(q-1)
. The following hold.

(a)

L(\chi_{\mathbf{a}},s)
converges for
s>D_{\mathbf{a}}
, and
L(\psi_{\mathbf{a}},s)
converges for
s>-D_{\mathbf{a}}
.

(b)

L(E_{\mathbf{a}}/K,\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}},s)
converges for
s>D_{\mathbf{a}}
, and
L(E_{\mathbf{a}}^{\vee}/K,\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}},s)
converges for
s>-D_{\mathbf{a}}
.

(c)

\displaystyle L(E_{\mathbf{a}}/K,\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}},s)=\prod_{b\in(A/fA)^{\times}}L(\chi_{\mathbf{a}}^{\rho_{b}},\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}},s)=L(\chi_{\mathbf{a}},\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}},s)^{r}
.

(d)

\displaystyle L(E_{\mathbf{a}}^{\vee}/K,\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}},s)=\prod_{b\in(A/fA)^{\times}}L(\psi_{\mathbf{a}}^{\rho_{b}},\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}},s)=L(\psi_{\mathbf{a}},\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}},s)^{r}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{p}
be a prime of
B
,
\mathfrak{p}\notin\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}}
, and set
\ell=[\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}:\mathbb{F}_{q}]
. By Theorem
3.4.7
(b) and §
3.4
, we see that for any
b\in(A/fA)^{\times}
,

\bigl\lvert\chi_{\mathbf{a}}^{\rho_{b}}(\mathfrak{p})\bigr\rvert_{\infty}={\lvert\widetilde{\theta}\rvert}_{\infty}^{\ell\deg\mathbf{a}}\quad\Rightarrow\quad\biggl\lvert\frac{\chi_{\mathbf{a}}^{\rho_{b}}(\mathfrak{p})}{\mathcal{N}(\mathfrak{p})^{s}}\biggr\rvert_{\infty}=\frac{{\lvert\widetilde{\theta}\rvert}_{\infty}^{\ell\deg\mathbf{a}}}{{\lvert\theta\rvert}_{\infty}^{s\ell}}={\lvert\theta\rvert}_{\infty}^{(1/(q-1)\cdot\deg\mathbf{a}-s)\ell}.

Taking
b=1
, this implies the first part of (a). Since
\psi_{\mathbf{a}}=\chi_{-\mathbf{a}}
, the second part also follows. The first equalities in both (c) and (d) follow immediately from Proposition
5.1.4
, and these equalities imply (b). It remains to show the second equalities in (c) and (d). Fix
\wp\in A_{+}
irreducible and relatively prime to the primes in
\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}}
. Using Proposition
5.1.4
, consider the (reciprocals of the) Euler factors in (
5.1.6
),

(5.1.11)

\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\mid\wp}\prod_{b\in(A/fA)^{\times}}\bigl(1-\chi_{\mathbf{a}}^{\rho_{b}}(\mathfrak{p})\mathcal{N}(\mathfrak{p})^{-s}\bigr)=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\mid\wp}\prod_{b\in(A/fA)^{\times}}\Bigl(1-\chi_{\mathbf{a}}(\rho_{b}(\mathfrak{p}))\wp^{-s[\mathbb{F}_{\wp}:\mathbb{F}_{q}]}\Bigr),

which follows from the definition of
\mathcal{N}(\mathfrak{p})
in §
2.4
and Lemma
3.4.11
. The Galois group
\Gal(K/k)
acts transitively on the primes above
\wp
in
B
with stabilizers of size
\delta=[\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}:\mathbb{F}_{\wp}]
for any
\mathfrak{p}\mid\wp
. Thus for each
\mathfrak{p}\mid\wp
, the multiset
\{\rho_{b}(\mathfrak{p})\mid b\in(A/fA)^{\times}\}
consists of the
r/\delta
primes above
\wp
, each with multiplicity
\delta
(as
\wp
is unramified in
B
). Therefore, the expression in (
5.1.11
) becomes

\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\mid\wp}\prod_{\mathfrak{q}\mid\wp}\Bigl(1-\chi_{\mathbf{a}}(\mathfrak{q})\wp^{-s[\mathbb{F}_{\wp}:\mathbb{F}_{q}]}\Bigr)^{\delta}=\prod_{\mathfrak{q}\mid\wp}\Bigl(1-\chi_{\mathbf{a}}(\mathfrak{q})\mathcal{N}(\mathfrak{q})^{-s}\Bigr)^{r},

where this equality follows from the observation that the inner product is independent of the outer product over
\mathfrak{p}\mid\wp
. This proves the second equality in (c), and the second equality in (d) follows immediately since
\psi_{\mathbf{a}}=\chi_{-\mathbf{a}}
.
∎

Remark 5.1.12
.

(a) As mentioned above, by Corollary
A.1.17
we can take
\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}}
to be exactly the primes dividing
f
, in which case identities in Proposition
5.1.10
simplify as

L(E_{\mathbf{a}}/K,s)=L(\chi_{\mathbf{a}},s)^{r},\quad L(E_{\mathbf{a}}^{\vee}/K,s)=L(\psi_{\mathbf{a}},s)^{r}.

(b) The proof of Proposition
5.1.10
(a) does not depend on
\mathbf{a}
being effective, and the conclusion holds for any
L(\chi_{\mathbf{a}},s)
.

5.2.
Taelman class module formulas

Continuing with the notation of the previous section, in §
5.3
we will utilize a theorem of Fang
[
23
]
*Thm. 1.10 to derive identities for
L(E_{\mathbf{a}}^{\vee},0)
and
L(\psi_{\mathbf{a}},0)
when
\mathbf{a}=[a/f]
is basic. In this section we review the essentials of Taelman’s results and Fang’s subsequent extensions.

In breakthrough work, Taelman
[
49
,
50
,
51
]
proved formulas for
L(\phi^{\vee}/F,0)
, where
\phi
is a Drinfeld module defined over a finite extension
F/k
. Taelman’s identities were generalized to abelian
t
-modules by Fang
[
23
]
, and later Anglès, Ngo Dac, Pellarin, and Tavares Ribeiro
[
6
,
7
,
8
,
9
,
10
]
have introduced and developed the theory of Stark units to extend these results greatly to the wider class of admissible
\mathbf{A}
-modules, for
\mathbf{A}
more general than
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
.

The formalism of these class formulas utilize two fundamental objects introduced by Taelman
[
50
,
51
]
, namely the regulator lattice and class module of a Drinfeld module, which were later generalized to
t
-modules. We will follow the setup from Anglès, Ngo Dac, and Tavares Ribeiro
[
6
,
7
,
8
]
. A third important object for
L
-values is the module of Stark units
[
6
,
7
,
8
,
9
,
10
]
, though we will ultimately not need it for our identities.

5.2.1.
Fitting ideals.

For a finite
A
-module
M
, we let
[M]_{A}\in A_{+}
denote the unique monic generator of the Fitting ideal of
M
. If
M\cong\oplus_{i=1}^{\ell}A/g_{i}A
for
g_{1},\dots,g_{\ell}\in A_{+}
, then
[M]_{A}=g_{1}\dots g_{\ell}
, which is independent of the choice of
g_{1},\dots,g_{\ell}
. If
M
is instead a finite
\mathbf{A}
-module, we will use the convention
[M]_{A}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=[M]_{\mathbf{A}}|_{t=\theta}
, which coerces the value into
A
. See for example
[
8
]
*§1.2 for more general properties.

5.2.2.
Lattices and covolumes.

We follow the constructions in
[
7
]
*§2.3
[
8
]
*§1.3, but see also
[
51
]
. Let
V
be a finite dimensional
k_{\infty}
-vector space of dimension
n\geqslant 1
. An
A
-lattice
in
V
is an
A
-submodule that is discrete and cocompact. An
A
-lattice
M\subseteq V
is necessarily free of rank
n
over
A
, and any
A
-basis of
M
is also a
k_{\infty}
-basis of
V
. If
M
,
N\subseteq V
are
A
-lattices, then we can find a
k_{\infty}
-isomorphism
\alpha:V\to V
such that
\alpha(M)\subseteq N
. The
covolume
of
N
in
M
is

[M:N]_{A}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{\det_{k_{\infty}}\alpha}{\sgn(\det_{k_{\infty}}\alpha)}\cdot\biggl[\frac{N}{\alpha(M)}\biggr]_{A}^{-1}\in k_{\infty}^{\times}.

By
[
6
]
*§2.3,
[M:N]_{A}
is independent of the choice of
\alpha
and matches the definition in
[
51
]
*Prop. 4. If
N\subseteq M
, then
[M:N]_{A}=[M/N]_{A}
. Generally,
[M:N]_{A}=[N:M]_{A}^{-1}
, and for any three
A
-lattices,
[M:N]_{A}=[M:P]_{A}\cdot[P:N]_{A}
. Furthermore, suppose
v_{1},\dots,v_{n}
form a
k_{\infty}
-basis of
V
and
m_{i}=\sum_{j}\beta_{ij}v_{j}
,
\beta_{ij}\in k_{\infty}
, form an
A
-basis of
N
. Then

(5.2.3)

[\oplus_{i=1}^{n}Av_{i}:N]_{A}=\frac{\det(\beta_{ij})}{\sgn(\det(\beta_{ij}))}.

If we let
\mathbf{k}_{\infty}={\mathbb{F}_{q}((t^{-1}))}
, then for a finite dimensional
\mathbf{k}_{\infty}
-vector space
V
, we can define
\mathbf{A}
-lattices
and covolumes in the same way by converting
\theta
to
t
in the above definitions.

5.2.4.
Taelman regulator for
E_{\mathbf{a}}
.

For
\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}_{f}
effective with
\deg\mathbf{a}>0
, let
E_{\mathbf{a}}:\mathbf{A}\to\Mat_{d}(B[\tau])
be a fixed model defined over
B
via Theorem
4.3.29
. The unit module
\mathrm{U}(E_{\mathbf{a}}/B)
and regulator
\mathrm{Reg}(E_{\mathbf{a}}/B)\in k_{\infty}^{\times}
are defined as in
[
8
]
*§2.1
[
50
]
*§5. Let

(5.2.5)

K_{\infty}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=K\otimes_{k}k_{\infty}\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}K_{\infty_{i}}.

Letting
I_{f}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{b\in A_{+}\mid\deg b<\deg f,(b,f)=1\}
, we have
\{\rho_{b}|_{K^{+}}:b\in I_{f}\}=\Gal(K^{+}/k)
as in §
3.2
. We use the convention that
\infty_{1}
-adic topology on
K
coincides with the
\infty
-adic subspace topology from
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
(so that
K_{\infty_{1}}=k_{\infty}(\zeta)\subseteq\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
), and we find
\{\infty_{1},\dots,\infty_{n}\}=\{\rho_{b}(\infty_{1})\mid b\in I_{f}\}
. Thus
K_{\infty}\cong\oplus_{b\in I_{f}}K_{\rho_{b}(\infty_{1})}
. In the usual way,
K\hookrightarrow K_{\infty}\to\oplus_{b\in I_{f}}K_{\rho_{b}(\infty_{1})}
via
\alpha\mapsto\alpha\otimes 1\mapsto(\rho_{b}(\alpha)\mid b\in I_{f})
. We let

\Exp_{E_{\mathbf{a}}}:\Lie(E_{\mathbf{a}})(K_{\infty})\to E_{\mathbf{a}}(K_{\infty})

be the
\mathbf{A}
-module map induced by
\exp_{E_{\mathbf{a}}}(\mathbf{z})
. For variables
\mathbf{z}_{b}
on
\Lie(E_{\mathbf{a}})(K_{\infty})
,

\Exp_{E_{\mathbf{a}}}(\mathbf{z}_{b}\mid b\in I_{f})=\bigl(\exp_{E_{\mathbf{a}}}^{\rho_{b}}(\mathbf{z}_{b})\mid b\in I_{f}\bigr),

where
\exp_{E_{\mathbf{a}}}^{\rho_{b}}(\mathbf{z})\in{K[[z_{1},\dots,z_{d}]]}^{d}
is obtained from
\exp_{E_{\mathbf{a}}}(\mathbf{z})
by applying
\rho_{b}
to its coefficients. As in
[
8
]
*§2.1, the
unit module
is

\mathrm{U}(E_{\mathbf{a}}/B)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Exp_{E_{\mathbf{a}}}^{-1}\bigl(E_{\mathbf{a}}(B)\bigr),

where on the right
E_{\mathbf{a}}(B)\subseteq E_{\mathbf{a}}(K_{\infty})
via
B\hookrightarrow K_{\infty}
.

Now
\Lie(E_{\mathbf{a}})(K_{\infty})
has the structure of a
k_{\infty}
-vector space with

(5.2.6)

\dim_{k_{\infty}}\Lie(E_{\mathbf{a}})(K_{\infty})=[K:k]\cdot\dim(E_{\mathbf{a}})=rd.

Also,
\Lie(E_{\mathbf{a}})(K_{\infty})
is an
\mathbf{A}
-module via
\mathrm{d}E_{\mathbf{a}}:\mathbf{A}\to\Mat_{d}(K)
. Letting
\mathbf{k}_{\infty}={\mathbb{F}_{q}((t^{-1}))}
, this map extends to
\mathrm{d}E_{\mathbf{a}}:\mathbf{k}_{\infty}\to\Mat_{d}(K_{\infty})
via

\mathrm{d}E_{\mathbf{a}}:\sum_{i=i_{0}}^{\infty}c_{i}t^{-i}\mapsto\sum_{i=i_{0}}^{\infty}c_{i}\bigl(\mathrm{d}E_{\mathbf{a},t}\bigr)^{-i},

which converges by
[
23
]
*Lem. 1.7. This makes
\Lie(E_{\mathbf{a}})(K_{\infty})
into a
\mathbf{k}_{\infty}
-vector space, also of dimension
rd
, in which
\Lie(E_{\mathbf{a}})(B)
is an
\mathbf{A}
-lattice
[
23
]
*Thm. 1.10. Thus,
\Lie(E_{\mathbf{a}})(B)
is simultaneously an
A
-lattice and an
\mathbf{A}
-lattice. When
\mathrm{d}E_{\mathbf{a},t}=\theta\mathrm{I}
, these two structures are essentially the same, up to interchanging the roles of
t
and
\theta
.

By
[
50
]
*Thm. 1
[
23
]
*Thm. 1.10, one finds that
\mathrm{U}(E_{\mathbf{a}}/B)
is an
\mathbf{A}
-lattice in
\Lie(E_{\mathbf{a}})(K_{\infty})
. The
regulator
of
E_{\mathbf{a}}
is

\mathrm{Reg}(E_{\mathbf{a}}/B)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\bigl[\Lie(E_{\mathbf{a}})(B):\mathrm{U}(E_{\mathbf{a}}/B)\bigr]_{\mathbf{A}}\big|_{t=\theta}\in k_{\infty}^{\times}.

5.2.7.
Class module of
E_{\mathbf{a}}
.

As noted in
[
8
]
*§2.1, the map
\Exp_{E_{\mathbf{a}}}
induces an exact sequence of
\mathbf{A}
-modules

0\to\mathrm{U}(E_{\mathbf{a}}/B)\to\Lie(E_{\mathbf{a}})(K_{\infty})\to\frac{E_{\mathbf{a}}(K_{\infty})}{E_{\mathbf{a}}(B)}\to\frac{E_{\mathbf{a}}(K_{\infty})}{E_{\mathbf{a}}(B)+\Exp_{E_{\mathbf{a}}}(\Lie(E_{\mathbf{a}})(K_{\infty}))}\to 0.

This last term

\mathrm{H}(E_{\mathbf{a}}/B)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{E_{\mathbf{a}}(K_{\infty})}{E_{\mathbf{a}}(B)+\Exp_{E_{\mathbf{a}}}(\Lie(E_{\mathbf{a}})(K_{\infty}))},

is the
class module
of
E_{\mathbf{a}}/B
. Fang
[
23
]
*Thm. 1.10 and Taelman
[
50
]
*Thm. 1 showed that it is a finite
\mathbf{A}
-module. We let

\mathrm{h}(E_{\mathbf{a}}/B)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=[\mathrm{H}(E_{\mathbf{a}}/B)]_{A}\quad(\in A_{+}).

The fundamental result is the class formula, which we have specialized to our setting.

Theorem 5.2.8

(Fang
[
23
]
*Thm. 1.10, Taelman
[
51
]
*Thm. 1)
.

Let
\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}_{f}
be effective with
\deg\mathbf{a}>0
, and let
E_{\mathbf{a}}:\mathbf{A}\to\Mat_{d}(B[\tau])
be a model of
E_{\mathbf{a}}
defined over
B
. Then

\prod_{\mathfrak{p}}\frac{[\Lie(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muE}_{\mathbf{a}})(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})]_{A}}{[\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muE}_{\mathbf{a}}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})]_{A}}=\mathrm{Reg}(E_{\mathbf{a}}/B)\cdot\mathrm{h}(E_{\mathbf{a}}/B),

where the product is taken over all primes of
B
and converges in
k_{\infty}^{\times}
.

5.3.
Special
L
-value identities for
E_{\mathbf{a}}

Let
\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}_{f}
be effective with
\deg\mathbf{a}>0
. We now analyze the terms in Theorem
5.2.8
in detail and use them to prove identities for
L(E_{\mathbf{a}}^{\vee},0)
and
L(\psi_{\mathbf{a}},0)
. At first we allow
\mathbf{a}
to be arbitrary, but for our main results, Theorem
5.3.15
and Corollary
5.3.17
, we require that
\mathbf{a}=[a/f]
be basic.

5.3.1.
Model for
E_{\mathbf{a}}
over
B
.

We assume that
E_{\mathbf{a}}:\mathbf{A}\to\Mat_{d}(B[\tau])
has been chosen in the following way. First we choose a model for
E_{\mathbf{a}}
using a normalized
\overline{k}[\sigma]
-basis defined over
K
as in Theorem
4.3.29
. Then similar to the beginning of §
4.4
, we choose
\alpha\in A_{+}
such that conjugating
E_{\mathbf{a}}
by
\alpha\mathrm{I}
yields a model defined over
B
. We require that for
\mathfrak{p}\in\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}}
we have
\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muE}_{\mathbf{a}}\cong\mathrm{d}\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muE}_{\mathbf{a}}
. Also, if
\mathfrak{p}\in\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}}
lies above above a prime
\wp
of
A
, then all of the primes in
B
above
\wp
are also in
\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}}
. These conditions can be achieved by building
\alpha
up by powers of primes in
A
one prime at a time.

5.3.2.
Local factors.

Let
\mathfrak{p}
be a prime of
B
, lying above
\wp\in A_{+}
. We investigate the terms in the product in Theorem
5.2.8
.

Lemma 5.3.3
.

For a prime
\mathfrak{p}
of
B
above a prime
\wp\in A_{+}
, the following hold.

(a)

For all such
\mathfrak{p}
,

\bigl[\Lie(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muE}_{\mathbf{a}})(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\bigr]_{A}=\wp^{d[\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}:\mathbb{F}_{\wp}]}.

(b)

For
\mathfrak{p}\notin\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}}
,

\bigl[\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muE}_{\mathbf{a}}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\bigr]_{A}=\gamma P_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}(1)=\gamma\prod_{b\in(A/fA)^{\times}}\bigl(1-\chi_{\mathbf{a}}^{\rho_{b}}(\mathfrak{p})\bigr),

where
\gamma\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}
is chosen to make the right-hand expressions monic.

Proof.

Part (a) is a straightforward modification of the proof of
[
22
]
*Prop. 4.10. As the eigenvalues of
\mathrm{d}\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muE}_{\mathbf{a},\wp(t)}
are all zero in
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}
, it follows that

\mathrm{d}\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muE}_{\mathbf{a},\wp(t)^{d}}=(\mathrm{d}\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muE}_{\mathbf{a},\wp(t)})^{d}=0.

Thus
\Lie(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muE}_{\mathbf{a}})(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})
is annihilated by
\wp(t)^{d}
. We then have an isomorphism of
\mathbf{A}
-modules
\Lie(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muE}_{\mathbf{a}})(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\cong\mathbf{A}/\wp(t)^{e_{1}}\mathbf{A}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathbf{A}/\wp(t)^{e_{m}}\mathbf{A}
with
e_{1}\leqslant\cdots\leqslant e_{m}\leqslant d
. On the one hand,

\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}\Lie(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muE}_{\mathbf{a}})(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})=d\cdot[\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}:\mathbb{F}_{q}]=d\cdot[\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}:\mathbb{F}_{\wp}]\cdot\deg\wp,

and on the other,
\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}\mathbf{A}/\wp(t)^{e_{1}}\mathbf{A}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathbf{A}/\wp(t)^{e_{m}}\mathbf{A}=(e_{1}+\cdots+e_{m})\deg\wp
.

In Proposition
5.1.4
, we observed that
P_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}(X)=\Char(\tau^{\ell},T_{\lambda}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muE}_{\mathbf{a}}),X)|_{t=\theta}
when
\mathfrak{p}
is a good prime. Part (b) follows from Proposition
5.1.4
combined with
[
33
]
*Cor. 3.7.8.
∎

Proposition 5.3.4
.

For
\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}_{f}
effective with
\deg\mathbf{a}>0
, fix a model
E_{\mathbf{a}}:\mathbf{A}\to\Mat_{d}(B[\tau])
as in §
5.3
. Then

\prod_{\mathfrak{p}}\frac{[\Lie(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muE}_{\mathbf{a}})(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})]_{A}}{[\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muE}_{\mathbf{a}}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})]_{A}}=L(E_{\mathbf{a}}^{\vee}/K,\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}},0)=L(\psi_{\mathbf{a}},\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}},0)^{r}.

Proof.

For
\mathfrak{p}\in\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}}
the term in the left-hand product is
1
since
\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muE}_{\mathbf{a}}\cong\mathrm{d}\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muE}_{\mathbf{a}}
. For
\mathfrak{p}\notin\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}}
, we have
\prod_{b\in(A/fA)^{\times}}\chi_{\mathbf{a}}^{\rho_{b}}(\mathfrak{p})=N^{K}_{k}(\chi_{\mathbf{a}}(\mathfrak{p}))
, which generates the ideal
N_{k}^{K}(\chi_{\mathbf{a}}(\mathfrak{p})B)\cap A
of
A
. By Theorem
3.4.7
(a), we see

N_{k}^{K}\bigl(\chi_{\mathbf{a}}(\mathfrak{p})B\bigr)\cap A=N^{K}_{k}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{\Theta_{\mathbf{a}}}\bigr)\cap A=\bigl(N^{K}_{k}(\mathfrak{p})\bigr)^{\Theta_{\mathbf{a}}}\cap A=\wp^{[\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}:\mathbb{F}_{\wp}]d}A.

Thus
N^{K}_{k}(\chi_{\mathbf{a}}(\mathfrak{p}))
equals
\wp^{[\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}:\mathbb{F}_{\wp}]d}
up to a constant from
\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}
. Lemma
5.3.3
then implies

\frac{[\Lie(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muE}_{\mathbf{a}})(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})]_{A}}{[\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muE}_{\mathbf{a}}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})]_{A}}=\prod_{b\in(A/fA)^{\times}}\frac{-\chi_{\mathbf{a}}^{\rho_{b}}(\mathfrak{p})}{1-\chi_{\mathbf{a}}^{\rho_{b}}(\mathfrak{p})}=\prod_{b\in(A/fA)^{\times}}\frac{1}{1-\psi_{\mathbf{a}}(\mathfrak{p})}=Q_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}(1)^{-1}.

As for what happened to
\gamma
from Lemma
5.3.3
(b), it has been accounted for with the minus sign in the middle term:
\sgn(-\chi_{\mathbf{a}}^{\rho_{b}}(\mathfrak{p})/(1-\chi_{\mathbf{a}}^{\rho_{b}}(\mathfrak{p})))=1
since
{\lvert\chi_{\mathbf{a}}^{\rho_{b}}(\mathfrak{p})\rvert}_{\infty}>1
by Theorem
3.4.7
(b). The result follows from (
5.1.6
) and Proposition
5.1.10
(d).
∎

5.3.5.
Regulator of
E_{\mathbf{a}}
.

Henceforth we will assume that
\mathbf{a}=[a/f]
is basic. In this case the dimension of
E_{\mathbf{a}}
is
d=r/(q-1)=n=[K^{+}:k]
by Lemma
4.1.2
(b). Also, Lemma
4.1.16
implies that the
\mathbf{A}
-module structure on
\Lie(E_{\mathbf{a}})(K_{\infty})
coincides with the scalar
A
-module structure on
K_{\infty}^{d}
. Recalling that we have constructed
E_{\mathbf{a}}
as in §
5.3
, the period lattice for this
E_{\mathbf{a}}
model is then
\alpha^{-1}\Lambda_{\mathbf{a}}
from Theorem
4.2.11
.

Recall from §
1.2
that
\Pi(x)\in k_{\infty}^{\times}
for
x\in k\setminus{A_{+}}
. Considering Theorem
4.2.11
(b), if

\mathrm{D}_{i}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\begin{pmatrix}\ddots&&\\
&\rho_{c}(\zeta)^{i-1}&\\
&&\ddots\end{pmatrix}_{c\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{a}}}\in\Mat_{d}(B),\quad\boldsymbol{\pi}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\begin{pmatrix}\vdots\\
\Pi\bigl([ca/f]\bigr)\\
\vdots\end{pmatrix}_{c\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{a}}}\in k_{\infty}^{d},

then for
1\leqslant i\leqslant r
, we have
\pi_{i}=\mathrm{D}_{i}\boldsymbol{\pi}
. Thus for
b\in I_{f}
, we can embed

\varepsilon_{b}:\alpha^{-1}\Lambda_{\mathbf{a}}\hookrightarrow K_{\rho_{b}(\infty_{1})}^{d}

via
a_{1}\alpha^{-1}\pi_{1}+\cdots+a_{r}\alpha^{-1}\pi_{r}\mapsto a_{1}\alpha^{-1}\rho_{b}(\mathrm{D}_{1})\boldsymbol{\pi}+\cdots+a_{r}\alpha^{-1}\rho_{b}(\mathrm{D}_{r})\boldsymbol{\pi}
. As in §
5.2
, taking
\varepsilon\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\oplus\varepsilon_{b}
we can then embed

(5.3.6)

\varepsilon:\bigl(\alpha^{-1}\Lambda_{\mathbf{a}}\bigr)^{d}\hookrightarrow K_{\infty}^{d}\cong\biggl(\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in A_{+}\\
\deg b<\deg f\\
(b,f)=1\end{subarray}}K_{\rho_{b}(\infty_{1})}^{d}\biggr).

Letting
\Lambda\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\im\varepsilon
, we note that
\Lambda\subseteq\ker\Exp_{E_{\mathbf{a}}}
, and thus

\Lambda\subseteq\mathrm{U}(E_{\mathbf{a}}/B).

Furthermore by (
5.2.6
),
\rank_{\mathbf{A}}\Lambda=rd=\dim_{k_{\infty}}\Lie(E_{\mathbf{a}})(K_{\infty})=\rank_{\mathbf{A}}\mathrm{U}(E_{\mathbf{a}}/B)
. Therefore,
\Lambda
has finite index in
\mathrm{U}(E_{\mathbf{a}}/B)
and is an
\mathbf{A}
-lattice in
\Lie(E_{\mathbf{a}})(K_{\infty})
.

Remark 5.3.7
.

It is here that
\mathbf{a}=[a/f]
being basic is significant. In (
5.3.6
), the tuples in
(\alpha^{-1}\Lambda_{\mathbf{a}})^{d}
are indexed by
b\in I_{f}
, which has
r/(q-1)
elements. However, the
d
in
K_{\infty}^{d}
on the right is coming from the dimension of
E_{\mathbf{a}}
. When
\mathbf{a}
is basic,
d=r/(q-1)
, and
\Lambda
is an
\mathbf{A}
-lattice. When
\mathbf{a}
is more general, one can still construct
\Lambda\subseteq\mathrm{U}(E_{\mathbf{a}}/B)
, but its rank over
\mathbf{A}
will be
r^{2}/(q-1)
, which generally will be strictly smaller than
rd
.

5.3.8.
The completion
k_{\infty}(\zeta)
.

Elements of
\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}\subseteq\Gal(K/k)
fix powers
\zeta^{m}
with
(q-1)\mid m
, and so in these cases
\zeta^{m}\in k_{\infty}
. Furthermore,
\{1,\zeta,\dots,\zeta^{q-2}\}
is a
k_{\infty}
-basis of
k_{\infty}(\zeta)
, as
[k_{\infty}(\zeta):k_{\infty}]=q-1
by §
3.2
, as well as a
k
-basis of
K^{+}=k(\zeta^{q-1})
.

Proposition 5.3.9
.

Let
\mathbf{a}=[a/f]\in\mathcal{A}_{f}
be basic. Fix a model
E_{\mathbf{a}}:\mathbf{A}\to\Mat_{d}(B[\tau])
chosen as in §
5.3
.

(a)

There is
C_{\mathrm{R}}\in\overline{k}^{\times}
with
\sgn(C_{\mathrm{R}})=1
and

C_{\mathrm{R}}^{r}\in\begin{cases}k^{\times}&\text{if $r$ is even,}\\
K^{\times}\ \text{and}\ C_{\mathrm{R}}^{2r}\in k^{\times}&\text{if $r$ is odd,}\end{cases}

such that

\mathrm{Reg}(E_{\mathbf{a}}/B)=C_{\mathrm{R}}^{r}\cdot\prod_{c\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{a}}}\Pi\bigl([ca/f]\bigr)^{r}.

(b)

Furthermore, if
f/(a,f)=f^{\prime}\in A_{+}
, then

\mathrm{Reg}(E_{\mathbf{a}}/B)=C_{\mathrm{R}}^{r}\cdot\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}b^{\prime}\in A_{+}\\
\deg b^{\prime}<\deg f^{\prime}\\
(b^{\prime},f^{\prime})=1\end{subarray}}\Pi(b^{\prime}/f^{\prime})^{[K_{f}:K_{f^{\prime}}]r}

Proof.

We continue with the notation of §
5.3
. From §
5.2
,

(5.3.10)

\mathrm{Reg}(E_{\mathbf{a}}/B)=\frac{[\Lie(E_{\mathbf{a}})(B):\Lambda]_{A}}{[\mathrm{U}(E_{\mathbf{a}}/B):\Lambda]_{A}},

with
[\mathrm{U}(E_{\mathbf{a}}/B):\Lambda]_{A}\in A_{+}
. We calculate
[\Lie(E_{\mathbf{a}})(B):\Lambda]_{A}
. An
\mathbf{A}
-basis for
\Lambda
consists of vectors of the form

(5.3.11)

\bigl(0,\dots,0,\alpha^{-1}\rho_{b}(\mathrm{D}_{i})\boldsymbol{\pi},0\dots,0\bigr)^{{\mathsf{T}}}\in\bigoplus_{b\in I_{f}}K_{\rho_{b}(\infty_{1})}^{d},

where the non-zero entry is in the
b
-th component. We consider first the component corresponding to
b=1
, which is simply
k_{\infty}(\zeta)^{d}
. By Theorem
4.2.11
(b) a short calculation yields that the block matrix in
\Mat_{r}(k_{\infty})
(with row blocks indexed by
c\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{a}})
,

M_{1}=\alpha^{-1}\Bigl(\Pi\bigl([ca/f]\bigr)\mathrm{I}_{q-1},\rho_{c}(\zeta)^{q-1}\Pi\bigl([ca/f]\bigr)\mathrm{I}_{q-1},\ldots,\rho_{c}(\zeta)^{(q-1)(d-1)}\Pi\bigl([ca/f]\bigr)\mathrm{I}_{q-1}\Bigr)_{c\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{a}}},

represents the elements
\alpha^{-1}\mathrm{D}_{1}\boldsymbol{\pi},\dots,\alpha^{-1}\mathrm{D}_{r}\boldsymbol{\pi}
of our
\mathbf{A}
-basis for
\Lambda
in
k_{\infty}(\zeta)^{d}
with respect to
\{1,\zeta,\dots,\zeta^{q-2}\}
. Properties of determinants yield

\det M_{1}=\alpha^{-r}\prod_{c\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{a}}}\Pi\bigl([ca/f]\bigr)^{q-1}\cdot\det\Bigl(\mathrm{I}_{q-1},\rho_{c}(\zeta)^{q-1}\mathrm{I}_{q-1},\dots,\rho_{c}(\zeta)^{(q-1)(d-1)}\mathrm{I}_{q-1}\Bigr)_{c\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{a}}}.

The determinant on the right is the determinant of the Kronecker product of the
d\times d
Vandermonde matrix
V_{1}
for
\{\rho_{c}(\zeta)^{q-1}\mid c\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{a}}\}
and
\mathrm{I}_{q-1}
. Thus,

\det M_{1}=\alpha^{-r}\det(V_{1})^{q-1}\prod_{c\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{a}}}\Pi\bigl([ca/f]\bigr)^{q-1}.

An observation from (
3.3.8
) and (
3.3.10
) is that

(5.3.12)

\{\rho_{c}|_{K^{+}}:c\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{a}}\}=\{\rho_{b}|_{K^{+}}:b\in I_{f}\}=\Gal(K^{+}/k)

(cf.
[
12
]
*Thm. 4.5.4), and so
\det V_{1}
is a square-root of the discriminant of the irreducible polynomial for
\zeta^{q-1}
over
k
. In particular
(\det V_{1})^{2}\in A
.

Now
\det M_{1}
is the determinant of the
b=1
component of the
\mathbf{A}
-basis in (
5.3.11
) in terms of the
k_{\infty}
-basis of
k_{\infty}(\zeta)^{d}=K_{\rho_{1}(\infty_{1})}^{d}
built from
\{1,\zeta,\dots,\zeta^{q-2}\}
. To consider the arbitrary
b
-th component in (
5.3.11
), the calculations are exactly the same, but with
\zeta
replaced by
\rho_{b}(\zeta)
. This yields a matrix
M_{b}\in\Mat_{r}(k_{\infty})
as above with Vandermonde matrix
V_{b}
for
\{\rho_{c}(\rho_{b}(\zeta))^{q-1}\mid c\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{a}}\}
such that

\det M_{b}=\alpha^{-r}\det(V_{b})^{q-1}\prod_{c\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{a}}}\Pi\bigl([ca/f]\bigr)^{q-1}.

By (
5.3.12
), we see that
\det V_{b}=\det V_{1}
up to
\pm 1
. Pulling everything together and using (
5.2.3
), we see that

(5.3.13)

\bigl[\Lie(E_{\mathbf{a}})(B):\Lambda\bigr]_{A}=\prod_{b\in I_{f}}\det M_{b}=\alpha^{-rd}\det(V_{1})^{r}\prod_{c\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{a}}}\Pi\bigl([ca/f]\bigr)^{r}.

Take
C_{\mathrm{R}}=\alpha^{-d}\det(V_{1})/[\mathrm{U}(E_{\mathbf{a}}/B):\Lambda]_{A}^{1/r}
chosen with sign
1
. We note
C_{\mathrm{R}}^{r}\in k^{\times}
when
r
is even, as
\det(V_{1})^{2}\in A
. Otherwise,
C_{\mathrm{R}}^{r}\in K^{\times}
and
C_{\mathrm{R}}^{2r}\in k^{\times}
, and (a) is done by (
5.3.10
).

For (b), if we write
a=\gamma a^{\prime}
, then by reduction modulo
f^{\prime}
,

\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{a}}=\{c\in A\mid\deg c<\deg f,\ (c,f)=1,\ \langle ca/f\rangle=1\}\\
\twoheadrightarrow\{c^{\prime}\in A\mid\deg c^{\prime}<\deg f^{\prime},\ (c^{\prime},f^{\prime})=1,\ \langle c^{\prime}a^{\prime}/f^{\prime}\rangle=1\},

and this map is
[K_{f}:K_{f^{\prime}}]
-to-one. Also, since
(a^{\prime},f^{\prime})=1
, if
b^{\prime}\in A_{+}
,
\deg b^{\prime}<\deg f^{\prime}
,
(b^{\prime},f^{\prime})=1
, then there a unique
c^{\prime}
in the target set such that
c^{\prime}a^{\prime}\bmod f^{\prime}=b^{\prime}
. In this case, if
c\mapsto c^{\prime}
, then
\Pi([ca/f])=\Pi([c^{\prime}a^{\prime}/f^{\prime}])=\Pi(b^{\prime}/f^{\prime})
, and the result follows.
∎

Remark 5.3.14
.

Taelman
[
50
]
*Rem. 4 observed that in the analogy with unit groups of number fields, his regulator also accounts for the square root of the discriminant in the usual residue formula for the Dedekind zeta function. We see this here as the constant
C_{\mathrm{R}}
contains terms coming from
\det V_{1}
, which is a
k^{\times}
-multiple of a square root of the discriminant of
K^{+}/k
(cf. Anderson’s
\Omega_{k}(n,\mathbf{a})
in
[
4
]
*Thm. 2.4).

Theorem 5.3.15
.

Let
\mathbf{a}=[a/f]\in\mathcal{A}_{f}
be basic, and fix
E_{\mathbf{a}}:\mathbf{A}\to\Mat_{d}(B[\tau])
as in §
5.3
. Let
C_{\mathrm{R}}
be chosen as in Proposition
5.3.9
, and pick
C_{\mathrm{H}}\in\overline{k}^{\times}
with
\sgn(C_{\mathrm{H}})=1
so that
\mathrm{h}(E_{\mathbf{a}}/B)=C_{\mathrm{H}}^{r}
. Then there exists
C_{\mathrm{E}}\in K^{\times}
with
C_{\mathrm{E}}^{r}\in k^{\times}
and
\sgn(C_{\mathrm{E}})=1
so that the following hold.

(a)

If
(a,f)=1
, then

L(E_{\mathbf{a}}^{\vee}/K,0)=C_{\mathrm{R}}^{r}\cdot C_{\mathrm{H}}^{r}\cdot C_{\mathrm{E}}^{r}\cdot\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in A_{+}\\
\deg b<\deg f\\
(b,f)=1\end{subarray}}\Pi(b/f)^{r}.

(b)

More generally, if
f/(a,f)=f^{\prime}\in A_{+}
, then

L(E_{\mathbf{a}}^{\vee}/K,0)=C_{\mathrm{R}}^{r}\cdot C_{\mathrm{H}}^{r}\cdot C_{\mathrm{E}}^{r}\cdot\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}b^{\prime}\in A_{+}\\
\deg b^{\prime}<\deg f^{\prime}\\
(b^{\prime},f^{\prime})=1\end{subarray}}\Pi(b^{\prime}/f^{\prime})^{[K_{f}:K_{f^{\prime}}]r}.

Proof.

Combining Theorem
5.2.8
with Propositions
5.3.4
and
5.3.9
, we obtain these same identities for
L(E_{\mathbf{a}}^{\vee}/K,\Sigma_{\mathbf{a}},0)
, but without the factor of
C_{\mathrm{E}}^{r}
, which comes from the extra Euler factors for
L(E_{\mathbf{a}}^{\vee}/K,0)
in (
5.1.8
). Then
C_{\mathrm{E}}\in K^{\times}
by (
5.1.9
) and Remark
5.1.12
.
∎

Remark 5.3.16
.

When
r
is even, we see that
L(E_{\mathbf{a}}^{\vee}/K,0)
is a
k^{\times}
-multiple of special
\Pi
-values. When
r
is odd, this multiple is a square root of an element of
k^{\times}
.

Our main corollary follows immediately from Remark
5.1.12
and Theorem
5.3.15
.

Corollary 5.3.17
.

Under the same conditions as Theorem
5.3.15
the following hold.

(a)

If
(a,f)=1
, then

L(\psi_{\mathbf{a}},0)=C_{\mathrm{R}}\cdot C_{\mathrm{H}}\cdot C_{\mathrm{E}}\cdot\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in A_{+}\\
\deg b<\deg f\\
(b,f)=1\end{subarray}}\Pi(b/f).

(b)

More generally, if
f/(a,f)=f^{\prime}\in A_{+}
, then

L(\psi_{\mathbf{a}},0)=C_{\mathrm{R}}\cdot C_{\mathrm{H}}\cdot C_{\mathrm{E}}\cdot\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}b^{\prime}\in A_{+}\\
\deg b^{\prime}<\deg f^{\prime}\\
(b^{\prime},f^{\prime})=1\end{subarray}}\Pi(b^{\prime}/f^{\prime})^{[K_{f}:K_{f^{\prime}}]}.

6.
Examples

We provide examples of Anderson Hecke characters, Sinha modules, and special
L
-values. One should compare with previous work in
[
1
]
*§6.6
[
12
]
*§6
[
48
]
*§3.3, and additional details can be found in
[
20
]
*§6.

6.1.
The case
\mathbf{a}=[1/\theta]

Taking
f=\theta
and
\mathbf{a}=[1/\theta]
, we have
\zeta=\zeta_{\theta}=(-\theta)^{1/(q-1)}
. We find that
\mathcal{D}_{\theta}(t,z)=t+z^{q-1}
. The Coleman function is

g_{[1/\theta]}=1-\frac{z}{\zeta}

with
W_{[1/\theta]}=0
and
\Xi_{[1/\theta]}=\xi
. In this case
B=\mathbb{F}_{q}[\zeta]
is a principal ideal domain, and after some calculations involving the techniques from §
3.4
, we find for
\mathfrak{p}=p(\zeta)B
a prime ideal of
B
with
p(\zeta)\in B_{+}
prime to
\theta
that

(6.1.1)

\psi_{[1/\theta]}(\mathfrak{p})=\frac{p(0)}{p}.

The
t
-comotive
H_{[1/\theta]}(\overline{k})
is simply
\overline{k}[t,z]
with the usual
\sigma
-action, and it has rank
q-1
, dimension
1
, and
\overline{k}[\sigma]
-basis
\{1\}
defined over
K
. We find (cf.
[
12
]
*§6.1) that

(6.1.2)

E_{[1/\theta],z}=\zeta-\zeta^{q}\tau,\quad E_{[1/\theta],t}=\theta(1+\theta\tau)^{q-1}.

Thus
E_{[1/\theta]}
is essentially a twist of the Carlitz module for
B
. One finds that

(6.1.3)

L(E_{[1/\theta]}^{\vee}/K,0)=\Pi(1/\theta)^{q-1},\quad L(\psi_{[1/\theta]},0)=\Pi(1/\theta),

and so the constants in Theorem
5.3.15
and Corollary
5.3.17
are all
1
. Although we omit the details, one way to prove these identities is to use specializations of Pellarin’s
L
-series for
\mathbf{B}=\mathbb{F}_{q}[z]
at
z=0
, since that is essentially how
\psi_{[1/\theta]}
is given in (
6.1.1
). Pellarin’s main identity
[
41
]
*Thm. 1 then leads to (
6.1.3
). We wonder if there is a more general connection between
L(\psi_{\mathbf{a}},0)
and deformation
L
-series as studied in
[
6
,
7
,
8
,
9
,
10
,
22
,
33
]
.

6.2.
The case
\mathbf{a}=[1/(\theta^{2}-\theta)]
,
q=3

Consider
f=\theta(\theta-1)
and
\mathbf{a}=[1/f]
when
q=3
. Then
K=k(\zeta_{f})
is biquadratic over
k
, containing intermediate quadratic fields
k(\zeta_{\theta})=k(\sqrt{-\theta})
,
k(\zeta_{\theta-1})=k(\sqrt{1-\theta})
, and
k(\sqrt{f})=K^{+}
. Moreover,

\mathcal{D}_{f}(t,z)=z^{4}+(t+1)z^{2}+1.

Since
t
is a rational expression of
z
in
\mathbf{K}=\mathbb{F}_{3}(t,z)
, we see that
\mathbf{K}=\mathbb{F}_{3}(z)
and that
X_{f}=\mathbb{P}^{1}
. Then
X_{f}
has two points at infinity,
\infty_{1}
,
\infty_{2}
, and
\divf(z)=\infty_{1}-\infty_{2}
, so
z\in\mathbf{B}^{\times}
. Moreover, since
t=-1-z^{2}-z^{-2}
, it follows that
\mathbf{B}=\mathbb{F}_{3}[z,z^{-1}]
. We find
W_{[1/f]}=\xi_{1}
,
\Xi_{[1/f]}=\xi_{1}+\xi_{\theta+1}
, and

g_{[1/f]}=(1-\zeta^{4})^{-1}(z-\zeta^{3})(z^{-1}+\zeta).

Using (
3.4.6
), if
\mathfrak{p}
is a prime ideal of
B=\mathbb{F}_{3}[\zeta,\zeta^{-1}]
generated by
p(\zeta)\in\mathbb{F}_{3}[\zeta]_{+}
, then

(6.2.1)

\psi_{[1/f]}(\mathfrak{p})=\frac{p(1)p(-1)p(\sqrt{-1})p(-\sqrt{-1})}{p(\zeta)p(-\zeta^{-1})}.

The
t
-comotive
H_{\mathbf{a}}(\overline{k})
is the ideal
H^{0}(\mathbf{U},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}(-\xi_{1}^{(1)}))=\overline{k}[z,z^{-1}](z-\zeta^{3})
, and it has rank
4
and dimension
2
. Using the techniques in §
4.3
, we obtain a
\overline{k}[\sigma]
-basis

(6.2.2)

\displaystyle h_{1}

\displaystyle=(1-\zeta^{4})^{-1}(z-\zeta^{3})(z^{-1}+\zeta)=g_{[1/f]},

\displaystyle\divf(h_{1})=\xi_{1}^{(1)}+\xi_{\theta+1}-\infty_{1}-\infty_{2},

\displaystyle h_{2}

\displaystyle=(1+\zeta^{2})^{-1}(z-\zeta^{3})(z^{-1}-\zeta^{-1}),

\displaystyle\divf(h_{2})=\xi_{1}^{(1)}+\xi_{1}-\infty_{1}-\infty_{2}.

We note that
h_{1}(\xi_{1})=1
,
h_{1}(\xi_{\theta+1})=0
,
h_{2}(\xi_{1})=0
, and
h_{2}(\xi_{\theta+1})=-(\theta+1)
. The last calculation implies that
\{h_{1},h_{2}\}
is almost but not exactly normalized in the sense of §
4.1
. However, this change simplified some of our calculations. We find that

(6.2.3)

\displaystyle E_{[1/f],z}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\zeta&0\\
0&-\zeta^{-1}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}\zeta-\zeta^{3}&-\zeta^{-5}(1-\zeta^{2})^{4}\\
-\zeta&\zeta^{-5}(1-\zeta^{2})^{3}\end{pmatrix}\tau,

\displaystyle E_{[1/f],z^{-1}}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\zeta^{-1}&0\\
0&-\zeta\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}\zeta^{-3}(1-\zeta^{2})&\zeta^{-3}(1-\zeta^{2})^{4}\\
\zeta^{-1}&\zeta^{-1}(1-\zeta^{2})^{3}\end{pmatrix}\tau,

and

(6.2.4)

E_{[1/f],t}=\begin{pmatrix}\theta&0\\
0&\theta\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}-\theta(\theta^{2}-1)&-\theta^{3}(\theta-1)\\
\theta(\theta-1)&\theta^{2}(\theta^{2}-1)\end{pmatrix}\tau\\
{}+\begin{pmatrix}\theta^{2}(\theta-1)^{2}(\theta^{2}+1)&-\theta^{6}(\theta^{3}-\theta)(\theta-1)\\
-\theta(\theta^{3}-\theta)(\theta-1)&\theta^{6}(\theta-1)^{2}(\theta^{2}+1)\end{pmatrix}\tau^{2}.

The determinant of the
\tau^{2}
coefficient of
E_{[1/f],t}
simplifies to
f^{8}
, and thus
E_{[1/f]}
is defined over
B
(in fact
E_{[1/f],t}
is defined over
A
itself) and is strictly pure in the sense of
[
39
]
*Ex. 3.38. Moreover, it follows that the reduction of
E_{[1/f]}
is good for every prime not dividing
f=\theta(\theta-1)
. We note that
[
12
]
*§6.2 indicates that
E_{[1/f]}
is isogenous to
E_{[1/\theta]}^{\oplus 2}
, which can be verified from (
6.2.4
) after a change of coordinates. For
L
-values we find after some calculation that

(6.2.5)

L(E_{[1/f]}^{\vee},0)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{=}}\frac{\theta^{2}(\theta-1)^{2}}{(\theta+1)^{4}}\cdot\Pi\biggl(\frac{1}{\theta(\theta-1)}\biggr)^{4}\Pi\biggl(\frac{\theta+1}{\theta(\theta-1)}\biggr)^{4},

and

(6.2.6)

L(\psi_{[1/f]},0)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{=}}\frac{\sqrt{\theta(\theta-1)}}{\theta+1}\cdot\Pi\biggl(\frac{1}{\theta(\theta-1)}\biggr)\Pi\biggl(\frac{\theta+1}{\theta(\theta-1)}\biggr),

where “
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{=}}
” represents that we have checked these identities numerically (with precision approximately
50
digits). We note that
\sqrt{\theta(\theta-1)}
is the square root of the discriminant of
K^{+}/k
, as we saw in Remark
5.3.14
.

Appendix A
Primes of good reduction

We prove here that the primes
\mathfrak{p}
of
B
not dividing
f
are primes of good reduction for
E_{\mathbf{a}}
, as mentioned in §
4.4
. The key arguments rely on analyses of the relationships between Sinha
t
-comotives over
K
and over
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}
.

A.1.
Good reduction of
H_{\mathbf{a}}
and
E_{\mathbf{a}}

We review some generalities for reductions of function spaces modulo
\mathfrak{p}
. For a divisor
D\in\Div_{K}(\mathbf{X})
such that
\mathcal{L}(D)\subseteq\overline{k}[t,z]
, we let

\mathcal{L}(D,B_{\mathfrak{p}})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathcal{L}(D)\cap B_{\mathfrak{p}}[t,z].

Because
D
is defined over
K
, as in §
3.4
it can be interpreted as a sum
\mathcal{D}
of horizontal divisors on the curve
\mathcal{X}=B_{\mathfrak{p}}\times_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}X
over
\Spec B_{\mathfrak{p}}
. As such we can identify
\mathcal{L}(D,B_{\mathfrak{p}})=H^{0}(\mathcal{X}/B_{\mathfrak{p}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}/B_{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathcal{D}))
, and moreover,

K\otimes_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{L}(D,B_{\mathfrak{p}})=\mathcal{L}(D)\cap K[t,z]=H^{0}(X/K,\mathcal{O}_{X/K}(D))=\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}\mathcal{L}(D,K).

The Riemann-Roch theorem
[
43
]
*Thm. 5.4 implies that
\mathcal{L}(D)
has a
\overline{k}
-basis consisting of elements in
K[t,z]
, so in particular

\mathcal{L}(D)=\overline{k}\otimes_{K}\mathcal{L}(D,K)=\overline{k}\otimes_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{L}(D,B_{\mathfrak{p}}).

Now let

\mathcal{L}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muD},\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=H^{0}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muD}))\cap\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}[t,z]=H^{0}(X/\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}},\mathcal{O}_{X/\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muD})),

and consider the reduction map

(A.1.1)

\pi:\mathcal{L}(D,B_{\mathfrak{p}})\to\mathcal{L}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muD},\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}).

Clearly,
\ker\pi=\mathcal{L}(D,B_{\mathfrak{p}})\cap\mathfrak{p}B_{\mathfrak{p}}[t,z]=\mathfrak{p}\mathcal{L}(D,B_{\mathfrak{p}})
. If we let
P=\im\pi
, then
P
is a finite dimensional
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}
-vector space, and

(A.1.2)

P\cong\mathcal{L}(D,B_{\mathfrak{p}})/\mathfrak{p}\mathcal{L}(D,B_{\mathfrak{p}}).

By Nakayama’s lemma
[
35
]
*§X.4, if we pick
\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{n}\in\mathcal{L}(D,B_{\mathfrak{p}})
such that
\overline{\alpha}_{1},\dots,\overline{\alpha}_{n}
is an
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}
-basis of
P
, then
\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{n}
are a
B_{\mathfrak{p}}
-basis of
\mathcal{L}(D,B_{\mathfrak{p}})
. Hence they also form a
K
-basis of
\mathcal{L}(D,K)
, and therefore,

(A.1.3)

\dim_{K}\mathcal{L}(D,K)\leqslant\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{L}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muD},\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}).

Now since
\mathbf{X}
is simply the extension of scalars to
\overline{k}
of the smooth projective irreducible curve
X/\mathbb{F}_{q}
, both
\mathbf{X}
and
X
have the same genus, say
\gamma
. In the case that
\deg D=\deg\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muD}>2\gamma-2
, the Riemann-Roch theorem assures us that (
A.1.3
) is an equality.

We apply these considerations to the situation of §
4.3
.
As in (
4.3.5
), for
i\in\mathbb{Z}
we let
M_{i}=\mathcal{L}(-W_{\mathbf{a}}^{(1)}+iI_{\mathbf{a}})
and
\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}=\mathcal{L}(-\overline{W}_{\mathbf{a}}^{(1)}+iI_{\mathbf{a}})
. We write

M_{i}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})=M_{i}\cap B_{\mathfrak{p}}[t,z]=\mathcal{L}(-W_{\mathbf{a}}^{(1)}+iI_{\mathbf{a}},B_{\mathfrak{p}}),\quad M_{i}(K)=M_{i}\cap K[t,z],

and

\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})=\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}\cap\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}[t,z]=\mathcal{L}(-\overline{W}_{\mathbf{a}}^{(1)}+iI_{\mathbf{a}},\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}).

We have reduction maps for each
i\in\mathbb{Z}
,

(A.1.4)

\pi_{i}:M_{i}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})\to\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}).

For
N\geqslant 0
taken from Lemma
4.3.6
(g) and its proof, we obtain that for
i\geqslant N-1
,

\dim_{K}M_{i}(K)=\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\quad\Rightarrow\quad\pi_{i}(M_{i}(B_{\mathfrak{p}}))=\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}).

In particular,

\pi_{N-1}(M_{N-1}(B_{\mathfrak{p}}))=\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{N-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}),\quad\pi_{N}(M_{N}(B_{\mathfrak{p}}))=\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{N}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}).

The maps
\{\pi_{i}\}
form maps of directed systems and induce

\pi:\bigcup_{i=0}^{\infty}M_{i}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})\to\bigcup_{i=0}^{\infty}\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}),\quad\bigl(=H_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\bigr).

We now prove a straightforward lemma on extending bases on two-dimensional arrays of vector spaces. Throughout we let ‘
\sqcup
’ denote a disjoint union.

Lemma A.1.5
.

Let
N\geqslant 0
, and let
\{V_{i,j}\mid 0\leqslant i\leqslant j\leqslant N\}
be (finite dimensional) vector spaces over a field
F
. Suppose we have injective
F
-linear maps

\displaystyle\rho_{i,j}:V_{i,j-1}

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow V_{i,j},\quad

\displaystyle(0\leqslant i<j\leqslant N),

\displaystyle\delta_{i,j}:V_{i-1,j}

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow V_{i,j},\quad

\displaystyle(0<i\leqslant j\leqslant N),

such that for
0<i<j\leqslant N
,

\delta_{i,j}\circ\rho_{i-1,j}=\rho_{i,j}\circ\delta_{i,j-1}:V_{i-1,j-1}\to V_{i,j},

and the induced map

V_{i-1,j-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}V_{i,j-1}\times_{V_{i,j}}V_{i-1,j}

is an isomorphism. Then for
0\leqslant i\leqslant j\leqslant N
, we can pick (finite) subsets
D_{i,j}
,
R_{i,j}
,
Z_{i,j}\subseteq V_{i,j}
such that the following hold.

(a)

Z_{i,j}
is a basis of
V_{i,j}
for
0\leqslant i\leqslant j\leqslant N
.

(b)

Z_{i,j}=\delta_{i,j}(Z_{i-1,j})\sqcup D_{i,j}
for
0<i\leqslant j\leqslant N
.

(c)

Z_{i,j}=\rho_{i,j}(Z_{i,j-1})\sqcup R_{i,j}
for
0\leqslant i<j\leqslant N
.

(d)

For
0<i<j\leqslant N
,

Z_{i,j}=(\delta_{i,j}\circ\rho_{i-1,j})(Z_{i-1,j-1})\sqcup\rho_{i,j}(D_{i,j-1})\sqcup\delta_{i,j}(R_{i-1,j})\sqcup(D_{i,j}\cap R_{i,j}).

Proof.

The diagram to keep in mind is the following,

(A.1.6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{0,0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{0,1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{0,2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{0,N}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{1,1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{1,2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{1,N}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{2,2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{2,N}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ddots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{N,N}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{0,1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{0,2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{1,1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{1,2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{1,N}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{1,2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{2,2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{2,N}}

For
0\leqslant i\leqslant j\leqslant N
, we define
D_{i,j}
,
R_{i,j}
, and
Z_{i,j}
recursively. We let
Z_{0,0}=D_{0,0}=R_{0,0}
be any basis of
V_{0,0}
. For
0<j\leqslant N
, if
Z_{0,j-1}
has been defined, we choose
R_{0,j}
so that
Z_{0,j}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\rho_{0,j}(Z_{0,j-1})\sqcup R_{0,j}
is a basis for
V_{0,j}
. For convenience we set
D_{0,j}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=R_{0,j}
. The sets
R_{0,j}
,
Z_{0,j}
for
0\leqslant j\leqslant N
then satisfy parts (a) and (c) as desired.
For
0<i\leqslant N
, suppose that
Z_{i-1,i}
has been defined. We pick
D_{i,i}\subseteq V_{i,i}
so that
Z_{i,i}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\delta_{i,i}(Z_{i-1,i})\sqcup D_{i,i}
is a basis of
V_{i,i}
. For convenience we set
R_{i,i}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=D_{i,i}
.

For
0<i<j\leqslant N
suppose that
Z_{i-1,j-1}
,
Z_{i,j-1}
,
Z_{i-1,j}
,
D_{i,j-1}
, and
R_{i-1,j}
have been defined. As such, by hypothesis

Z_{i,j-1}=\delta_{i,j-1}(Z_{i-1,j-1})\sqcup D_{i,j-1},\quad Z_{i-1,j}=\rho_{i-1,j}(Z_{i-1,j-1})\sqcup R_{i-1,j}.

Consider the augmented square from (
A.1.6
),
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{i-1,j-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{i-1,j}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{i,j-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{i,j}(V_{i,j-1})+\delta_{i,j}(V_{i-1,j})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{i,j}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{i-1,j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta{i,j-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{i,j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{i,j}}

Because
V_{i-1,j-1}\cong V_{i,j-1}\times_{V_{i,j}}V_{i-1,j}
, we conclude that

\rho_{i,j}(V_{i,j-1})\cap\delta_{i,j}(V_{i-1,j})=(\delta_{i,j}\circ\rho_{i-1,j})(V_{i-1,j-1}).

Thus
(\delta_{i,j}\circ\rho_{i-1,j})(Z_{i-1,j-1})\sqcup\rho_{i,j}(D_{i,j-1})\sqcup\delta_{i,j}(R_{i-1,j})
is a basis of
\rho_{i,j}(V_{i,j-1})+\delta_{i,j}(V_{i-1,j})
, and so we can pick
Y\subseteq V_{i,j}
so that

Z_{i,j}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(\delta_{i,j}\circ\rho_{i-1,j})(Z_{i-1,j-1})\sqcup\rho_{i,j}(D_{i,j-1})\sqcup\delta_{i,j}(R_{i-1,j})\sqcup Y

is a basis for
V_{i,j}
. We then set

D_{i,j}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\rho_{i,j}(D_{i,j-1})\sqcup Y,\quad R_{i,j}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\delta_{i,j}(R_{i-1,j})\sqcup Y.

In particular,
D_{i,j}=\rho_{i,j}(D_{i,j-1})\sqcup(D_{i,j}\cap R_{i,j})
and
R_{i,j}=\delta_{i,j}(R_{i-1,j})\sqcup(D_{i,j}\cap R_{i,j})
.
Properties (b)–(d) then follow.
∎

Proposition A.1.7
.

Let
\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}_{f}
be effective with
\deg\mathbf{a}>0
, and let
\mathfrak{p}
be a maximal ideal of
B
relatively prime to
f
. Then
H_{\mathbf{a}}(\overline{k})
has a
\overline{k}[\sigma]
-basis
\{h_{1},\dots,h_{d}\}\subseteq B_{\mathfrak{p}}[t,z]
such that
\{\overline{h}_{1},\dots,\overline{h}_{d}\}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}[t,z]
is an
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}[\sigma]
-basis of
H_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})
.

Proof.

We construct our
\overline{k}[\sigma]
-basis of
H_{\mathbf{a}}(\overline{k})
through Proposition
4.3.2
, namely using the recursion in §
4.3
and Remark
4.3.28
.
We first define a sequence
\{\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
of
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}
-subspaces of
H_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})
. For
i<0
, we set
\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{i}=\{0\}
, and as in (
A.1.4
), for
i\geqslant 0
we take

(A.1.8)

\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{i}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\pi(M_{i}(B_{\mathfrak{p}}))=\pi_{i}(M_{i}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})).

We consider the following diagram of finite dimensional
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}
-vector spaces, where for
0\leqslant i\leqslant j\leqslant N
, the
i
-th row is filtration of
\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})
and the
j
-th column is a filtration of
\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{j}
,

(A.1.9)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{0}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\cap\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{0}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\cap\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{0}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\cap\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{N}=\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{0}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\cap\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\cap\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{N}=\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\cap\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{N}=\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ddots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{N}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\cap\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{N}=\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{N}.}

We are in the situation of Lemma
A.1.5
with
V_{i,j}=\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\cap\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{j}
. The maps
\delta_{i,j}
,
\rho_{i,j}
, in Lemma
A.1.5
are inclusions and can be suppressed. Thus, for
0\leqslant i\leqslant j\leqslant N
, we select

\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muD}_{i,j},\ \mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muR}_{i,j},\ \mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muZ}_{i,j}\subseteq\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\cap\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{j},

such that
\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muZ}_{i,j}
is an
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}
-basis of
\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\cap\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{j}
and
\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muD}_{i,j}
,
\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muR}_{i,j}
satisfy the conclusions in Lemma
A.1.5
. Accordingly,
\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muZ}_{0,0}=\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muR}_{0,0}
, and after repeated applications of Lemma
A.1.5
(b)–(c),

\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muZ}_{i,j}=\biggl(\bigsqcup_{0<\ell\leqslant j}\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muR}_{0,\ell}\biggr)\sqcup\biggl(\bigsqcup_{0<k\leqslant i}\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muD}_{k,j}\biggr),\quad(0\leqslant i\leqslant j\leqslant N).

In particular for
0\leqslant i\leqslant N
,

\displaystyle\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{i}=\biggl(\bigoplus_{0\leqslant\ell\leqslant i}\Span_{\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muR}_{0,\ell})\biggr)\oplus\biggl(\bigoplus_{0<k\leqslant i}\Span_{\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muD}_{k,i})\biggr),

\displaystyle\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})=\biggl(\bigoplus_{0\leqslant\ell\leqslant N}\Span_{\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muR}_{0,\ell})\biggr)\oplus\biggl(\bigoplus_{0<k\leqslant i}\Span_{\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muD}_{k,N})\biggr)=\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{i}\oplus\biggl(\bigoplus_{i<\ell\leqslant N}\Span_{\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muR}_{i,\ell})\biggr).

Now as
\{\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\}
satisfies the criteria of §
4.3.8
, we can construct an
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}[\sigma]
-basis of
H_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})
using Proposition
4.3.2
, namely the construction in §
4.3
together with Lemma
4.3.12
. Following along with §
4.3
, we construct
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}
-bases
\{\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muS}_{i}\}
and
\{\overline{T}_{i}\}
. We can take

\overline{T}_{0}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muR}_{0,0}\sqcup\dots\sqcup\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muR}_{0,N},

and we set
\overline{T}_{0,j}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muR}_{0,j}
for
0\leqslant j\leqslant N
.
By the construction in §
4.3
, for
i>0
,
\overline{T}_{i}
is selected by choosing an
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}
-basis of
\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})/(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})+\sigma\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))
.
We have inclusions

\frac{\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{i}+(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})+\sigma\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))}{\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})+\sigma\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})}\subseteq\frac{(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\cap\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{i+1})+(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})+\sigma\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))}{\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})+\sigma\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})}\\
{}\subseteq\dots\subseteq\frac{(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\cap\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{N})+(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})+\sigma\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))}{\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})+\sigma\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})}=\frac{\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})}{\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})+\sigma\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})}.

From this we see that we can select
\overline{T}_{i}
to be a union of subsets of bases of

\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{i}\subseteq(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\cap\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{i+1})\subseteq\cdots\subseteq(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\cap\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{N-1})\subseteq\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}).

However, since
\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})+\sigma\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\supseteq\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})
, these subsets do not come from the rows
<i
in (
A.1.9
). Therefore, for
i\leqslant j\leqslant N
, using Lemma
A.1.5
(d), we can go back and choose
\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muD}_{i,j}
,
\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muR}_{i,j}
, with

(A.1.10)

\overline{T}_{i,j}\subseteq\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muD}_{i,j}\cap\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muR}_{i,j}\quad\textup{so that}\quad\overline{T}_{i}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\overline{T}_{i,i}\sqcup\cdots\sqcup\overline{T}_{i,N}

induces an
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}
-basis of
\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})/(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})+\sigma\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))
as desired. By §
4.3
and Remark
4.3.28
,
\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muS}_{0}=\overline{T}_{0}=\overline{T}_{0,0}\sqcup\cdots\sqcup\overline{T}_{0,N}
, and for
1\leqslant i\leqslant N
,

\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muS}_{i}=\bigsqcup_{0\leqslant k\leqslant i}\overline{T}_{k}=\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leqslant k\leqslant i\\
k\leqslant\ell\leqslant N\end{subarray}}\overline{T}_{k,\ell}\quad\Rightarrow\quad\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muS}_{N}=\bigsqcup_{0\leqslant k\leqslant\ell\leqslant N}\overline{T}_{k,\ell}.

In particular
\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muS}_{N}
is an
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}[\sigma]
-basis of
H_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})
.

Now we consider
M_{i}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})
for
0\leqslant i\leqslant N
, and the
B_{\mathfrak{p}}
-submodules, for
0\leqslant i\leqslant j\leqslant N
,

(A.1.11)

\mathcal{M}_{i,j}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\pi_{j}^{-1}\bigl(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\bigr)=\pi_{j}^{-1}\bigl(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\cap\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{j}\bigr)\subseteq M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}}).

We can fashion a similar diagram to (
A.1.9
) by placing
\mathcal{M}_{i,j}
in each entry, and we note from the definition of
\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{i}
that

\mathcal{M}_{i,i}=\pi_{i}^{-1}\bigl(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\cap\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{i}\bigr)=M_{i}(B_{\mathfrak{p}}),\quad 0\leqslant i\leqslant N.

Furthermore, for
0\leqslant i\leqslant j\leqslant N
,

\pi_{j}(\mathcal{M}_{i,j})=\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\cap\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{j},

as
\pi_{j}
surjects onto
\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{j}
by construction. For
0\leqslant j\leqslant N
, by the discussion in (
A.1.2
), Nakayama’s lemma implies that
M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})
is a free
B_{\mathfrak{p}}
-module of finite rank, and

\rank_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})=\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{j},\quad(0\leqslant j\leqslant N).

More generally, for
0\leqslant i\leqslant j\leqslant N
, we have the exact sequence of
B_{\mathfrak{p}}
-modules,

(A.1.12)

0\to\mathcal{M}_{i,j}\cap\mathfrak{p}M_{j}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\to\mathcal{M}_{i,j}\xrightarrow{\pi_{j}}\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\cap\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{j}\to 0,

and

(A.1.13)

\frac{\mathcal{M}_{i,j}}{\mathcal{M}_{i,j}\cap\mathfrak{p}M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}\cong\frac{\mathcal{M}_{i,j}+\mathfrak{p}M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}{\mathfrak{p}M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}\subseteq\frac{M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}{\mathfrak{p}M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}.

Thus,

\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\biggl(\frac{\mathcal{M}_{i,j}+\mathfrak{p}M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}{\mathfrak{p}M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}\biggr)=\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\bigl(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\cap\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{j}\bigr)\leqslant\rank_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}}).

For
0\leqslant i\leqslant j\leqslant N
, we set

\mathcal{V}_{i,j}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{\mathcal{M}_{i,j}+\mathfrak{p}M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}{\mathfrak{p}M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}=\frac{\pi_{j}^{-1}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))+\mathfrak{p}M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}{\mathfrak{p}M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})},

and we note

\mathcal{V}_{i,i}=\frac{M_{i}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})+\mathfrak{p}M_{i}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}{\mathfrak{p}M_{i}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}=\frac{M_{i}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}{\mathfrak{p}M_{i}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})},\quad(0\leqslant i\leqslant N).

We define
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}
-linear maps

\rho_{i,j}:\mathcal{V}_{i,j-1}\to\mathcal{V}_{i,j},\ (0\leqslant i<j\leqslant N),\quad\delta_{i,j}:\mathcal{V}_{i-1,j}\to\mathcal{V}_{i,j},\ (0<i\leqslant j\leqslant N),

by the natural maps

\displaystyle\rho_{i,j}

\displaystyle:\frac{\pi_{j-1}^{-1}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))+\mathfrak{p}M_{j-1}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}{\mathfrak{p}M_{j-1}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}\to\frac{\pi_{j}^{-1}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))+\mathfrak{p}M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}{\mathfrak{p}M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})},

\displaystyle\delta_{i,j}

\displaystyle:\frac{\pi_{j}^{-1}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))+\mathfrak{p}M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}{\mathfrak{p}M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}\to\frac{\pi_{j}^{-1}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))+\mathfrak{p}M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}{\mathfrak{p}M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}.

It is clear that
\delta_{i,j}
is injective for
0<i\leqslant j\leqslant N
. Furthermore, for
0\leqslant i<j\leqslant N
,

\bigl(\pi_{j-1}^{-1}\bigl(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\bigr)+\mathfrak{p}M_{j-1}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})\bigr)\cap\mathfrak{p}M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})\subseteq M_{j-1}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})\cap\mathfrak{p}M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})=\mathfrak{p}M_{j-1}(B_{\mathfrak{p}}),

so
\rho_{i,j}
is injective. Now for
0<i<j\leqslant N
, we have
\delta_{i,j}\circ\rho_{i-1,j}=\rho_{i,j}\circ\delta_{i,j-1}:\mathcal{V}_{i-1,j-1}\to\mathcal{V}_{i,j}
, and we consider the induced map,
\phi:\mathcal{V}_{i-1,j-1}\to\mathcal{V}_{i,j-1}\times_{\mathcal{V}_{i,j}}\mathcal{V}_{i-1,j}
. Moreover, we have

\phi:\frac{\pi_{j-1}^{-1}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))+\mathfrak{p}M_{j-1}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}{\mathfrak{p}M_{j-1}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}\\
\to\frac{\pi_{j-1}^{-1}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))+\mathfrak{p}M_{j-1}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}{\mathfrak{p}M_{j-1}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}\times_{\mathcal{V}_{i,j}}\frac{\pi_{j}^{-1}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))+\mathfrak{p}M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}{\mathfrak{p}M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})},

where the right-hand term consists of pairs
(\alpha,\beta)
for which
\rho_{i,j}(\alpha)=\delta_{i,j}(\beta)
, and for
x\in\pi_{j-1}^{-1}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))
,

\phi\bigl(x+\mathfrak{p}M_{j-1}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})\bigr)=\bigl(x+\mathfrak{p}M_{j-1}(B_{\mathfrak{p}}),x+\mathfrak{p}M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})\bigr).

We see easily that
\phi
is injective. To see that
\phi
is surjective, suppose that
y\in\pi_{j-1}^{-1}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))
and
z\in\pi_{j}^{-1}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))
satisfy
\rho_{i,j}(y+\mathfrak{p}M_{j-1}(B_{\mathfrak{p}}))=\delta_{i,j}(z+\mathfrak{p}M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}}))
.
That is,

y+\mathfrak{p}M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})=z+\mathfrak{p}M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}}).

But then
\pi_{j}(z)=\pi_{j}(y)=\pi_{j-1}(y)
. Since
\pi_{j}(z)\in\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})
and
\pi_{j-1}(y)\in\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})
, we see that
\pi_{j-1}(y)\in\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})
, and so
y\in\pi_{j-1}^{-1}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))
. Furthermore,

\phi\bigl(y+\mathfrak{p}M_{j-1}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})\bigr)=\bigl(y+\mathfrak{p}M_{j-1}(B_{\mathfrak{p}}),y+\mathfrak{p}M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})\bigr)=\bigl(y+\mathfrak{p}M_{j-1}(B_{\mathfrak{p}}),z+\mathfrak{p}M_{j}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})\bigr),

and thus
\phi
is surjective.

Therefore, the
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}
-vector spaces
\{\mathcal{V}_{i,j}\mid 0\leqslant i\leqslant j\leqslant N\}
together with corresponding maps
\delta_{i,j}
,
\rho_{i,j}
, satisfy the hypotheses of Lemma
A.1.5
. Furthermore, the isomorphisms

\mathcal{V}_{i,j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\cap\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{j},

induced by (
A.1.12
)–(
A.1.13
), are compatible so that

(A.1.14)

\bigl\{\mathcal{V}_{i,j}\mid 0\leqslant i\leqslant j\leqslant N\bigr\}\quad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longleftrightarrow}}\quad\bigl\{\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}\cap\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{j}\mid 0\leqslant i\leqslant j\leqslant N\bigr\}

are isomorphic systems satisfying the hypotheses of Lemma
A.1.5
. In this way, the
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}
-bases
\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muZ}_{i,j}
obtained from Lemma
A.1.5
for
\{\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\cap\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{j}\}
can be transferred to
\{\mathcal{V}_{i,j}\}
with the same properties (and vice versa). Furthermore, for
0\leqslant i\leqslant N
, the inclusions
\mathcal{V}_{0,i}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1,i}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{i,i}
are

\frac{\pi_{i}^{-1}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{0}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))+\mathfrak{p}M_{i}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}{\mathfrak{p}M_{i}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}\subseteq\frac{\pi_{i}^{-1}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))+\mathfrak{p}M_{i}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}{\mathfrak{p}M_{i}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\frac{M_{i}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}{\mathfrak{p}M_{i}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}.

Applying Nakayama’s lemma we find subsets
D_{k,i}\subseteq\pi_{i}^{-1}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{k}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))
for
0\leqslant k\leqslant i
so that

(A.1.15)

Z_{i}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=D_{0,i}\sqcup\cdots\sqcup D_{i,i}

is a
B_{\mathfrak{p}}
-basis of
M_{i}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})
with
\pi(Z_{i})=\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muZ}_{i,i}
. Since
\pi_{i}(\pi_{i}^{-1}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{k}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})))=\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{k}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\cap\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{i}
, these can be arranged so that

\pi_{i}(D_{k,i})=\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muD}_{k,i},\quad(0\leqslant k\leqslant i\leqslant N).

Before the final analysis, we need to investigate compatibility with the bases
\overline{T}_{i}
coming from
\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})/(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})+\sigma\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))
. To that end, we recall from Lemma
4.3.6
(c) and Remark
4.3.11
that, for
i>0
,
M_{i-1}+\sigma M_{i-1}
is defined over
K
, and moreover,

M_{i-1}+\sigma M_{i-1}=\mathcal{L}(-W_{\mathbf{a}}^{(1)}+(i-1)I_{\mathbf{a}})+\mathcal{L}(-W_{\mathbf{a}}^{(1)}-\Xi_{\mathbf{a}}+iI_{\mathbf{a}}).

We set

\displaystyle(M_{i-1}+\sigma M_{i-1})(B_{\mathfrak{p}})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(M_{i-1}+\sigma M_{i-1})\cap B_{\mathfrak{p}}[t,z],

\displaystyle(M_{i-1}+\sigma M_{i-1})(K)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(M_{i-1}+\sigma M_{i-1})\cap K[t,z],

and note that since
M_{i-1}+\sigma M_{i-1}
is defined over
K
,

(M_{i-1}+\sigma M_{i-1})(K)=K\otimes_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}(M_{i-1}+\sigma M_{i-1})(B_{\mathfrak{p}}).

We claim that the
B_{\mathfrak{p}}
-modules

\frac{M_{i}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}{M_{i-1}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})},\quad\frac{M_{i}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}{(M_{i-1}+\sigma M_{i-1})(B_{\mathfrak{p}})},

are torsion free. Let us consider the second module. Suppose that
h\in M_{i}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})
and
u\in B_{\mathfrak{p}}
with
uh\in(M_{i-1}+\sigma M_{i-1})(B_{\mathfrak{p}})
. Then
h\in(M_{i-1}+\sigma M_{i-1})(K)
, and so

h\in(M_{i-1}+\sigma M_{i-1})(K)\cap M_{i}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})\\
=(M_{i-1}+\sigma M_{i-1})(K)\cap M_{i}(K)\cap B_{\mathfrak{p}}[t,z]=(M_{i-1}+\sigma M_{i-1})(B_{\mathfrak{p}}).

The argument for the first module is similar. In particular, as
M_{i-1}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})\subseteq(M_{i-1}+\sigma M_{i-1})(B_{\mathfrak{p}})\subseteq M_{i}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})
are all free
B_{\mathfrak{p}}
-modules of finite rank, we find that a
B_{\mathfrak{p}}
-basis for
M_{i-1}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})
can be extended to bases of both
(M_{i-1}+\sigma M_{i-1})(B_{\mathfrak{p}})
and
M_{i}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})
.

We can construct the sets
\{S_{i}\}
,
\{T_{i}\}
, in §
4.3
by using
B_{\mathfrak{p}}
-bases of
M_{i}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})/(M_{i-1}+\sigma M_{i-1})(B_{\mathfrak{p}})
. Moreover, we have a filtration,

\frac{\pi_{i}^{-1}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{k}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))+(M_{i-1}+\sigma M_{i-1})(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}{(M_{i-1}+\sigma M_{i-1})(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}\subseteq\frac{M_{i}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}{(M_{i-1}+\sigma M_{i-1})(B_{\mathfrak{p}})},\quad(0\leqslant k\leqslant i),

of free
B_{\mathfrak{p}}
-modules of finite rank. For
0\leqslant k\leqslant i
, since
\pi_{i}(\pi_{i}^{-1}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{k}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})))=\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{k}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\cap\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{i}
, it follows from Nakayama’s lemma that

\rank_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}\biggl(\frac{\pi_{i}^{-1}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{k}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))+(M_{i-1}+\sigma M_{i-1})(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}{(M_{i-1}+\sigma M_{i-1})(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}\biggr)\\
\geqslant\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\biggl(\frac{(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{k}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\cap\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muP}_{i})+(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})+\sigma\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))}{\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})+\sigma\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{i-1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})}\biggr).

Therefore, after perhaps rearranging our
B_{\mathfrak{p}}
-basis
Z_{i}
of
M_{i}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})
from (
A.1.15
), we can pick a subset
T_{k,i}\subseteq Z_{i}
which induces a
B_{\mathfrak{p}}
-basis of
(\pi_{i}^{-1}(\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muM}_{k}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))+(M_{i-1}+\sigma M_{i-1})(B_{\mathfrak{p}}))/(M_{i-1}

+\sigma M_{i-1})(B_{\mathfrak{p}}))
, such that

(A.1.16)

\pi_{i}(T_{k,i})\supseteq\overline{T}_{k,i}.

Moreover, we can take
T_{i}=T_{0,i}\sqcup\dots\sqcup T_{i,i}
to be our choice of subset of
M_{i}
that induces a
\overline{k}
-basis of
M_{i}/(M_{i-1}+\sigma M_{i-1})
as in §
4.3
.

As noted,
\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muS}_{N}
is an
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}[\sigma]
-basis of
H_{\mathbf{a},\mathfrak{p}}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})
. Proposition
4.3.2
and Remark
4.3.28
imply

S_{N}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\bigsqcup_{0\leqslant i\leqslant N}T_{i}=\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leqslant i\leqslant N\\
0\leqslant k\leqslant i\end{subarray}}T_{k,i}=\bigsqcup_{0\leqslant k\leqslant i\leqslant N}T_{k,i}

is a
\overline{k}[\sigma]
-basis of
H_{\mathbf{a}}(\overline{k})
. But then by (
4.4.6
),

d=|S_{N}|=\sum_{i=0}^{N}\sum_{k=0}^{i}|T_{k,i}|\geqslant\sum_{i=0}^{N}\sum_{k=0}^{i}|\overline{T}_{k,i}|=|\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muS}_{N}|=d.

Therefore, this inequality is an equality, and the containments in (
A.1.16
) are also equalities. Furthermore,
S_{N}
satisfies
\pi(S_{N})=\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5muS}_{N}
, and we are done.
∎

The following corollary is then a consequence of Propositions
4.4.8
and
A.1.7
.

Corollary A.1.17
.

Let
\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}_{f}
be effective with
\deg\mathbf{a}>0
, and let
\mathfrak{p}
be a maximal ideal of
B
relatively prime to
f
. Then
E_{\mathbf{a}}
has good reduction at
\mathfrak{p}
.
